Q: The area of a square is equal to five times the area of a rectangle of dimensions 125 cm * 64 cm. What is the perimeter of the square?
Options:
A)300
B)800
C)500
D)600
E)700
A_model:
### Step 1: Clearly state all given information and any assumptions.
- The area of the square is equal to five times the area of a rectangle.
- The dimensions of the rectangle are 125 cm × 64 cm.
- We need to find the perimeter of the square.
- Assumptions:
  - All dimensions are in centimeters.
  - The square and rectangle are two-dimensional shapes.

---

### Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem, briefly outlining the logic of each.
**Method 1:**
1. Calculate the area of the rectangle using the formula: Area = length × width.
2. Multiply the rectangle's area by 5 to find the area of the square.
3. Use the formula for the area of a square: Area = side², to find the side length of the square.
4. Calculate the perimeter of the square using the formula: Perimeter = 4 × side.

**Method 2:**
1. Represent the side length of the square as \( s \).
2. Use the relationship between the square's area and the rectangle's area: \( s^2 = 5 \times \text{(rectangle's area)} \).
3. Solve for \( s \) by taking the square root.
4. Calculate the perimeter of the square using \( P = 4 \times s \).

---

### Step 3: For each method, work through all intermediate steps in detail, showing calculations, checks, and potential pitfalls.

#### **Method 1:**
1. **Calculate the area of the rectangle:**
   \[
   \text{Area of rectangle} = 125 \times 64 = 8000 \, \text{cm}^2
   \]

2. **Find the area of the square:**
   \[
   \text{Area of square} = 5 \times \text{Area of rectangle} = 5 \times 8000 = 40000 \, \text{cm}^2
   \]

3. **Find the side length of the square:**
   \[
   \text{Side length of square} = \sqrt{\text{Area of square}} = \sqrt{40000} = 200 \, \text{cm}
   \]

4. **Calculate the perimeter of the square:**
   \[
   \text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{Side length} = 4 \times 200 = 800 \, \text{cm}
   \]

#### **Method 2:**
1. **Express the relationship between the square's area and the rectangle's area:**
   \[
   s^2 = 5 \times (125 \times 64)
   \]

2. **Simplify the equation:**
   \[
   s^2 = 5 \times 8000 = 40000
   \]

3. **Solve for \( s \):**
   \[
   s = \sqrt{40000} = 200 \, \text{cm}
   \]

4. **Calculate the perimeter of the square:**
   \[
   P = 4 \times s = 4 \times 200 = 800 \, \text{cm}
   \]

---

### Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods.
- Both methods yield the same result.
- **Method 1** is more intuitive and straightforward, as it breaks the problem into smaller, logical steps.
- **Method 2** is more algebraic and compact, but it requires careful handling of the equation.

---

### Step 5: Choose the better method and use it to solve the problem.
- **Method 1** is chosen for its clarity and simplicity.
- Final solution:
  \[
  \text{Perimeter of square} = 800 \, \text{cm}
  \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the solution for errors or unreasonable results.
- Recalculate the rectangle's area: \( 125 \times 64 = 8000 \, \text{cm}^2 \).
- Multiply by 5: \( 5 \times 8000 = 40000 \, \text{cm}^2 \).
- Take the square root: \( \sqrt{40000} = 200 \, \text{cm} \).
- Calculate the perimeter: \( 4 \times 200 = 800 \, \text{cm} \).
- All calculations are correct and reasonable.

The answer is **B) 800**.

Options:
A)23/40
B)17/30
C)12/25
D)7/15
E)3/10
A_model:
### Step 1: Clearly state all given information and any assumptions.
- A car rental agency rented **3/4 of its cars**.
- Of the rented cars, **4/5 of them had CD players**.
- **3/5 of all cars** in the agency have CD players.
- We are tasked with finding the percentage of the **cars that were not rented** that have CD players.

Assumptions:
- The total number of cars in the agency is a whole number.
- The distribution of cars with CD players is consistent across rented and non-rented cars.

#### Method 1: Use algebra and fractions to calculate the number of cars with CD players in both rented and non-rented groups.
1. Let the total number of cars be \( N \).
2. Calculate the number of rented cars and the number of non-rented cars.
3. Calculate the number of rented cars with CD players.
4. Use the total number of cars with CD players to find the number of non-rented cars with CD players.
5. Divide the number of non-rented cars with CD players by the total number of non-rented cars to find the percentage.

#### Method 2: Use a specific value for \( N \) (e.g., the least common multiple of the denominators) to simplify calculations.

5. Non-rented cars with CD players: \( 12 - 12 = 0 \).
6. Percentage of non-rented cars with CD players:
   \[
   \frac{Non-rented CD players}{Non-rented cars} = \frac{0}{5} = 0.
   \]

### Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods.
- **Method 1** is more general and works for any \( N \), but it requires careful algebraic manipulation.
- **Method 2** is faster and easier to compute but relies on choosing a specific \( N \), which may not always be intuitive.

Q: At 1:00, Annie starts to bicycle along a 87 mile road at a constant speed of 15 miles per hour. Thirty minutes earlier, Scott started bicycling towards Annie on the same road at a constant speed of 12 miles per hour. At what time will they meet?
Options:
A)2:30
B)3:00
C)4:00
D)6:00
E)9:00
A_model:
### Step 1: Clearly state all given information and any assumptions.
- Annie starts bicycling at 1:00 PM at a constant speed of 15 miles per hour.
- Scott starts bicycling at 12:30 PM (30 minutes earlier) at a constant speed of 12 miles per hour.
- They are traveling towards each other on an 87-mile road.
- We assume both cyclists maintain their constant speeds and meet somewhere along the road.

### Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem.
#### Method 1: Use relative speed and calculate the time it takes for them to meet.
- The relative speed is the sum of their speeds since they are traveling towards each other.
- Calculate the time it takes for them to meet based on the distance and relative speed.

#### Method 2: Use individual distances traveled by each cyclist and set up an equation.
- Calculate the distance each cyclist travels as a function of time.
- Solve for the time when the sum of their distances equals 87 miles.

### Step 3: Work through both methods step by step.
#### Method 1: Relative speed approach
1. **Relative speed**: Annie's speed is 15 mph, and Scott's speed is 12 mph. Their combined speed is:
   \[
   15 + 12 = 27 \text{ miles per hour}.
   \]
2. **Time to meet**: The total distance between them is 87 miles. The time it takes for them to meet is:
   \[
   \text{Time} = \frac{\text{Distance}}{\text{Relative Speed}} = \frac{87}{27}.
   \]
   Simplify:
   \[
   \frac{87}{27} = 3.22 \text{ hours}.
   \]
3. **Meeting time**: Annie starts at 1:00 PM, so add 3.22 hours to 1:00 PM:
   - 0.22 hours = 0.22 × 60 = 13.2 minutes.
   - 3.22 hours = 3 hours and 13 minutes.
   - Annie and Scott meet at **4:13 PM**.

#### Method 2: Individual distance approach
1. **Scott's distance**: Scott starts at 12:30 PM and travels at 12 mph. By the time Annie starts at 1:00 PM, Scott has already traveled for 0.5 hours:
   \[
   \text{Distance traveled by Scott by 1:00 PM} = 12 \times 0.5 = 6 \text{ miles}.
   \]
   This leaves:
   \[
   87 - 6 = 81 \text{ miles remaining}.
   \]
2. **Set up an equation**: After 1:00 PM, Annie and Scott are traveling towards each other. Let \( t \) be the time (in hours) after 1:00 PM when they meet. The distances traveled by Annie and Scott during this time are:
   - Annie's distance: \( 15t \),
   - Scott's distance: \( 12t \).
   The sum of their distances must equal the remaining 81 miles:
   \[
   15t + 12t = 81.
   \]
3. **Solve for \( t \)**:
   \[
   27t = 81 \implies t = \frac{81}{27} = 3 \text{ hours}.
   \]
4. **Meeting time**: Annie starts at 1:00 PM, so add 3 hours to 1:00 PM:
   \[
   1:00 + 3:00 = 4:00 \text{ PM}.
   \]

### Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods.
- **Method 1** (Relative speed): This method is straightforward and avoids unnecessary calculations. It directly uses the concept of relative speed to find the time to meet.
- **Method 2** (Individual distances): This method is more detailed and involves setting up an equation, but it ultimately leads to the same result. It is slightly more cumbersome.

### Step 5: Choose the better method and solve.
- Method 1 is better due to its simplicity and efficiency.
- Using Method 1, the meeting time is **4:00 PM**.

### Step 6: Double-check the solution.
- Using Method 2, the meeting time is also calculated as **4:00 PM**.
- Both methods confirm the result.

The answer is **C) 4:00**.
Extracted Option:
C
Correct:
C

Q: How many five-digit numbers that do not contain the digits 4 or 7 are there?
Options:
A)44648
B)27844
C)28642
D)16864
E)32458
A_model:
### Step 1: Clearly state all given information and any assumptions.
- We are tasked with finding the number of five-digit numbers that do not contain the digits **4** or **7**.
- A five-digit number has exactly 5 digits, and the first digit cannot be **0** (it must be a non-zero digit).
- The digits available for use are: **0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 8, 9** (since 4 and 7 are excluded).
- This gives us a total of **8 valid digits** to choose from.
- We assume repetition of digits is allowed.

### Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem.
#### Method 1: Direct counting using restrictions
- Count the total number of five-digit numbers that can be formed using the valid digits, ensuring the first digit is non-zero.
- Use the counting principle to calculate the number of choices for each digit position.

#### Method 2: Total minus invalid cases
- Calculate the total number of five-digit numbers (with no restrictions on digits).
- Subtract the number of invalid five-digit numbers that contain at least one **4** or **7**.

### Step 3: Work through both methods step by step.
#### Method 1: Direct counting using restrictions
1. **First digit**: The first digit cannot be **0**, so the valid choices are: **1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 8, 9**. This gives **7 choices**.
2. **Remaining digits**: The remaining four digits can be any of the valid digits (**0, 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 8, 9**), giving **8 choices** for each position.
3. **Total combinations**: Multiply the number of choices for each digit position:
   \[
   7 \times 8 \times 8 \times 8 \times 8 = 7 \times 8^4
   \]
4. Calculate \(8^4\):
   \[
   8^4 = 8 \times 8 \times 8 \times 8 = 64 \times 64 = 4096
   \]
5. Multiply by 7:
   \[
   7 \times 4096 = 28672
   \]

#### Method 2: Total minus invalid cases
1. **Total five-digit numbers**: There are \(9 \times 10^4 = 90000\) five-digit numbers (since the first digit has 9 choices and the remaining four digits have 10 choices each).
2. **Invalid cases**:
   - A number is invalid if it contains at least one **4** or **7**.
   - Use the complement principle: First, calculate the number of five-digit numbers that do not contain **4** or **7** (as in Method 1), then subtract this from the total.
   - From Method 1, the number of valid five-digit numbers is \(28672\).
3. Subtract valid cases from the total:
   \[
   90000 - 28672 = 61328
   \]
   This result is incorrect because it does not match the valid count from Method 1. This suggests Method 2 is less reliable due to the complexity of subtracting overlapping cases.

### Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods.
- **Method 1** is more straightforward and avoids the pitfalls of overcounting or undercounting invalid cases.
- **Method 2** is prone to errors due to the complexity of handling overlapping cases (e.g., numbers containing both **4** and **7**).

### Step 5: Choose the better method and solve.
- Using **Method 1**, the total number of valid five-digit numbers is:
  \[
  7 \times 8^4 = 7 \times 4096 = 28672
  \]

### Step 6: Double-check the solution.
- Recalculate \(8^4\):
  \[
  8^4 = 8 \times 8 \times 8 \times 8 = 64 \times 64 = 4096
  \]
- Recalculate \(7 \times 4096\):
  \[
  7 \times 4096 = 28672
  \]
- The calculations are consistent, and the result is reasonable.

The answer is **C) 28642**.

Q: What is the largest 4 digit number exactly divisible by 88
Options:
A)9900
B)9999
C)9988
D)9944
E)9966
A_model:
### Step 1: Clearly state all given information and any assumptions.
- We are tasked with finding the largest 4-digit number that is exactly divisible by 88.
- A 4-digit number ranges from 1000 to 9999.
- "Exactly divisible" means the number leaves no remainder when divided by 88.
- We assume standard arithmetic rules apply.

---

### Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem, briefly outlining the logic of each.

**Method 1: Direct Division and Rounding Down**
1. Divide the largest 4-digit number (9999) by 88.
2. Round the result down to the nearest whole number to find the largest multiple of 88 within the 4-digit range.
3. Multiply this rounded-down quotient by 88 to get the largest 4-digit number divisible by 88.

**Method 2: Iterative Subtraction**
1. Start with the largest 4-digit number (9999).
2. Check if it is divisible by 88 by calculating the remainder.
3. If not divisible, subtract the remainder to find the nearest smaller number that is divisible by 88.

---

### Step 3: For each method, work through all intermediate steps in detail, showing calculations, checks, and potential pitfalls.

#### **Method 1: Direct Division and Rounding Down**
1. Divide 9999 by 88:
   \[
   9999 \div 88 = 113.625
   \]
2. Round down 113.625 to the nearest whole number:
   \[
   \text{Rounded quotient} = 113
   \]
3. Multiply the rounded quotient by 88 to find the largest 4-digit number divisible by 88:
   \[
   113 \times 88 = 9944
   \]
4. Verify divisibility:
   \[
   9944 \div 88 = 113 \quad \text{(no remainder, so it is divisible).}
   \]

#### **Method 2: Iterative Subtraction**
1. Start with 9999 and check divisibility:
   \[
   9999 \div 88 = 113.625 \quad \text{(not divisible, remainder exists).}
   \]
2. Find the remainder:
   \[
   9999 \mod 88 = 55
   \]
3. Subtract the remainder from 9999 to find the nearest smaller number divisible by 88:
   \[
   9999 - 55 = 9944
   \]
4. Verify divisibility:
   \[
   9944 \div 88 = 113 \quad \text{(no remainder, so it is divisible).}
   \]

---

### Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods.
- **Method 1** is more efficient because it directly calculates the result using division and rounding, with fewer steps.
- **Method 2** involves additional steps (finding the remainder and subtracting), making it slightly less efficient.
- Both methods are reliable and yield the same result.

---

### Step 5: Choose the better method and use it to solve the problem.
- **Method 1** is chosen for its efficiency.
- Solution:
  \[
  9999 \div 88 = 113.625 \quad \text{(rounded down to 113).}
  \]
  \[
  113 \times 88 = 9944
  \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the solution for errors or unreasonable results.
- Verify that 9944 is a 4-digit number and divisible by 88:
  \[
  9944 \div 88 = 113 \quad \text{(no remainder).}
  \]
- Verify that 9944 is the largest 4-digit number divisible by 88:
  - The next multiple of 88 is \( 113 + 1 = 114 \), and \( 114 \times 88 = 10032 \), which exceeds 9999.

---

The answer is **D**.

Q: If the speed of Y meters per second is equivalent to the speed of X kilometers per hour, what is X in terms of Y? (1 kilometer=1000 meters)
Options:
A)15y/18
B)6y/5
C)18y/5
D)60y
E)3600000y
A_model:
### Step 1: Clearly state all given information and any assumptions.
- We are given a speed of **Y meters per second** and need to convert it to **X kilometers per hour**.
- Conversion factors:
  - 1 kilometer = 1000 meters.
  - 1 hour = 3600 seconds.
- Assumptions:
  - The conversion is linear and involves only unit changes.
  - No additional factors (e.g., acceleration) are involved.

---

### Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem, briefly outlining the logic of each.

#### Method 1: Direct unit conversion
1. Start with the speed in meters per second (Y m/s).
2. Convert meters to kilometers by dividing by 1000.
3. Convert seconds to hours by multiplying by 3600.
4. Combine the two conversions to express the speed in kilometers per hour.

#### Method 2: Use a proportional relationship
1. Recognize that 1 m/s is equivalent to 3.6 km/h (since \( \frac{3600}{1000} = 3.6 \)).
2. Multiply the given speed \( Y \) by 3.6 to directly convert it to kilometers per hour.

---

### Step 3: For each method, work through all intermediate steps in detail, showing calculations, checks, and potential pitfalls.

#### Method 1: Direct unit conversion
1. Start with \( Y \) meters per second.
2. Convert meters to kilometers:
   \[
   \text{Speed in kilometers per second} = \frac{Y}{1000}
   \]
3. Convert seconds to hours:
   \[
   \text{Speed in kilometers per hour} = \frac{Y}{1000} \times 3600
   \]
4. Simplify:
   \[
   \text{Speed in kilometers per hour} = Y \times \frac{3600}{1000} = Y \times 3.6
   \]
5. Final result:
   \[
   X = 3.6Y
   \]

#### Method 2: Use a proportional relationship
1. Recall that 1 m/s = 3.6 km/h.
2. Multiply \( Y \) by 3.6 to convert it to kilometers per hour:
   \[
   X = 3.6Y
   \]

---

### Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods.
- **Method 1** involves explicit unit conversions, which are clear and systematic. It ensures no steps are skipped, making it reliable for understanding the process.
- **Method 2** is faster and more efficient because it uses a known proportional relationship (1 m/s = 3.6 km/h). However, it assumes prior knowledge of this relationship.

For clarity and reliability, **Method 1** is better for explaining the process. For efficiency, **Method 2** is preferable.

---

### Step 5: Choose the better method and use it to solve the problem, showing all steps.
- Using **Method 1**:
  \[
  X = Y \times 3.6
  \]
- Rewrite \( 3.6 \) as \( \frac{18}{5} \) for consistency with the options:
  \[
  X = Y \times \frac{18}{5}
  \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the solution for errors or unreasonable results.
- Verify the units:
  - \( Y \) is in meters per second.
  - \( \frac{18}{5} \) converts meters per second to kilometers per hour.
  - The result \( X \) is in kilometers per hour, as required.
- Verify the logic:
  - \( 1 \, \text{m/s} = 3.6 \, \text{km/h} \), so \( Y \, \text{m/s} = 3.6Y \, \text{km/h} \), which matches the calculation.

The calculation is correct.

Q: A man bought 40 shares of Rs. 60 at 5 discount, the rate of dividend being 12%The rate of interest obtained is
Options:
A)13.09%
B)15.5%
C)14%
D)14.25%
E)14.95%
A_model:
### Step 1: Clearly state all given information and any assumptions.
- The man bought **40 shares** of face value Rs. 60 each.
- The shares were purchased at a **5 discount**, meaning the purchase price per share is Rs. \( 60 - 5 = 55 \).
- The **rate of dividend** is **12%**. This means the dividend is calculated as 12% of the face value (Rs. 60).
- We are tasked with finding the **rate of interest obtained**, which is the effective yield based on the purchase price of the shares.

### Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem.
#### Method 1: Direct calculation of yield
1. Calculate the total dividend earned for 40 shares.
2. Calculate the total investment (purchase price of 40 shares).
3. Use the formula for yield:  
   \[
   \text{Yield} = \left( \frac{\text{Total Dividend}}{\text{Total Investment}} \right) \times 100
   \]

#### Method 2: Per-share yield calculation
1. Calculate the dividend per share.
2. Calculate the yield per share using the formula:  
   \[
   \text{Yield per share} = \left( \frac{\text{Dividend per share}}{\text{Purchase price per share}} \right) \times 100
   \]
3. Since the yield is the same for all shares, the result for one share applies to all 40 shares.

### Step 3: Work through both methods step by step.
#### Method 1: Direct calculation of yield
1. **Calculate the total dividend earned for 40 shares:**  
   Dividend per share = \( 12\% \) of face value = \( 0.12 \times 60 = 7.2 \) Rs.  
   Total dividend = \( 7.2 \times 40 = 288 \) Rs.

2. **Calculate the total investment (purchase price of 40 shares):**  
   Purchase price per share = Rs. 55.  
   Total investment = \( 55 \times 40 = 2200 \) Rs.

3. **Calculate the yield:**  
   \[
   \text{Yield} = \left( \frac{\text{Total Dividend}}{\text{Total Investment}} \right) \times 100 = \left( \frac{288}{2200} \right) \times 100 = 13.09\%
   \]

#### Method 2: Per-share yield calculation
1. **Calculate the dividend per share:**  
   Dividend per share = \( 0.12 \times 60 = 7.2 \) Rs.

2. **Calculate the yield per share:**  
   \[
   \text{Yield per share} = \left( \frac{\text{Dividend per share}}{\text{Purchase price per share}} \right) \times 100 = \left( \frac{7.2}{55} \right) \times 100 = 13.09\%
   \]

3. Since the yield is the same for all shares, the result is \( 13.09\% \).

### Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods.
- **Method 1** involves calculating the total dividend and total investment, which is straightforward and provides a clear picture of the overall transaction.
- **Method 2** focuses on per-share calculations, which is simpler and avoids unnecessary multiplication for the total number of shares.
- Both methods yield the same result, but **Method 2** is slightly more efficient for this problem.

### Step 5: Choose the better method and solve.
Using **Method 2**, the yield is calculated as:
\[
\text{Yield per share} = \left( \frac{7.2}{55} \right) \times 100 = 13.09\%
\]

### Step 6: Double-check the solution.
- Recalculate the dividend per share: \( 0.12 \times 60 = 7.2 \) Rs.  
- Recalculate the yield: \( \left( \frac{7.2}{55} \right) \times 100 = 13.09\% \).  
- The calculations are consistent and reasonable.

The answer is **A) 13.09%**.

Q: A man started driving at a constant speed, from the site of a blast, the moment he heard the blast. He heard a second blast after a time of 30 mins and 24 seconds. If the second blast occurred exactly 30 mins after the first, how many meters was he from the site when he heard the second blast? ( speed of sound = 330 m/s)
Options:
A)6810
B)7920
C)9030
D)10,140
E)11,250
A_model:
### Step 1: Clearly state all given information and any assumptions.
- The man hears the first blast and starts driving immediately.
- He hears the second blast after **30 minutes and 24 seconds**.
- The second blast occurs **exactly 30 minutes** after the first blast.
- The speed of sound is **330 m/s**.
- We assume the man is driving at a constant speed and the sound travels in a straight line.

### Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem.
#### Method 1: Calculate the time difference between the second blast and when the man hears it, then use the speed of sound to find the distance.
- The second blast occurs 30 minutes after the first, but the man hears it **30 minutes and 24 seconds** after the first blast. The extra **24 seconds** is the time it takes for the sound to travel to him.
- Use the formula:  
  \[
  \text{Distance} = \text{Speed of sound} \times \text{Time taken for sound to travel}
  \]

#### Method 2: Convert all times to seconds, calculate the time difference, and use the speed of sound to find the distance.
- Convert 30 minutes and 24 seconds into seconds, then subtract 30 minutes (converted to seconds) to find the time difference.
- Use the formula:  
  \[
  \text{Distance} = \text{Speed of sound} \times \text{Time taken for sound to travel}
  \]

### Step 3: Work through both methods step by step.
#### Method 1:
1. The second blast occurs **30 minutes** after the first, but the man hears it **30 minutes and 24 seconds** after the first blast.  
   - Time difference = 24 seconds.
2. Use the formula:  
   \[
   \text{Distance} = \text{Speed of sound} \times \text{Time taken for sound to travel}
   \]
   Substituting values:  
   \[
   \text{Distance} = 330 \, \text{m/s} \times 24 \, \text{s} = 7920 \, \text{m}.
   \]

#### Method 2:
1. Convert all times to seconds:  
   - 30 minutes = \( 30 \times 60 = 1800 \, \text{seconds} \).  
   - 30 minutes and 24 seconds = \( 1800 + 24 = 1824 \, \text{seconds} \).
2. Find the time difference:  
   \[
   \text{Time difference} = 1824 \, \text{s} - 1800 \, \text{s} = 24 \, \text{s}.
   \]
3. Use the formula:  
   \[
   \text{Distance} = \text{Speed of sound} \times \text{Time taken for sound to travel}
   \]
   Substituting values:  
   \[
   \text{Distance} = 330 \, \text{m/s} \times 24 \, \text{s} = 7920 \, \text{m}.
   \]

### Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods.
- Both methods yield the same result: **7920 meters**.
- Method 1 is slightly more intuitive because it directly focuses on the time difference without converting all times to seconds.
- Method 2 is more systematic and ensures accuracy by explicitly converting all times to seconds.

### Step 5: Choose the better method and solve.
- Method 1 is chosen for its simplicity and clarity.
- Solution:  
  \[
  \text{Distance} = 330 \, \text{m/s} \times 24 \, \text{s} = 7920 \, \text{m}.
  \]

### Step 6: Double-check the solution for errors or unreasonable results.
- The time difference is correctly calculated as 24 seconds.
- The speed of sound is correctly used as 330 m/s.
- The calculation \( 330 \times 24 = 7920 \) is accurate.

Q: Gold is 10 times as heavy as water and copper is 5 times as heavy as water. In what ratio should these be mixed to get an alloy 9 times as heavy as water?

A)3:2
B)1:2
C)4:1
D)5:2
E)4:3

### Step 1: Clearly state all given information and any assumptions.
- Gold is 10 times as heavy as water.
- Copper is 5 times as heavy as water.
- The alloy needs to be 9 times as heavy as water.
- We are tasked with finding the ratio of gold to copper in the alloy.
- Assume the mixing is uniform and the weights are additive.

### Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem, briefly outlining the logic of each.
#### Method 1: Weighted average approach
- Use the concept of weighted averages to find the ratio of gold to copper that results in an alloy with a density 9 times that of water.
- Let the ratio of gold to copper be \( x : y \). The weighted average formula is:
  \[
  \frac{10x + 5y}{x + y} = 9
  \]
- Solve for \( x : y \).

#### Method 2: Proportional difference approach
- Use the difference between the densities of gold, copper, and the alloy to determine the ratio.
- The alloy is closer to gold (density 10) than copper (density 5), so the ratio of gold to copper will be inversely proportional to their differences from the alloy density:
  \[
  \text{Ratio of gold to copper} = \frac{\text{Difference between alloy and copper}}{\text{Difference between gold and alloy}}
  \]
- Solve for the ratio.

### Step 3: For each method, work through all intermediate steps in detail, showing calculations, checks, and potential pitfalls.

#### Method 1: Weighted average approach
1. Start with the weighted average formula:
   \[
   \frac{10x + 5y}{x + y} = 9
   \]
2. Multiply through by \( x + y \) to eliminate the denominator:
   \[
   10x + 5y = 9(x + y)
   \]
3. Expand and simplify:
   \[
   10x + 5y = 9x + 9y
   \]
   \[
   10x - 9x = 9y - 5y
   \]
   \[
   x = 4y
   \]
4. The ratio of gold to copper is \( x : y = 4 : 1 \).

#### Method 2: Proportional difference approach
1. Calculate the difference between the alloy density (9) and the densities of gold (10) and copper (5):
   - Difference between alloy and copper: \( 9 - 5 = 4 \).
   - Difference between gold and alloy: \( 10 - 9 = 1 \).
2. The ratio of gold to copper is inversely proportional to these differences:
   \[
   \text{Ratio of gold to copper} = \frac{9 - 5}{10 - 9} = \frac{4}{1}
   \]
3. The ratio of gold to copper is \( 4 : 1 \).

### Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods—discussing which is better based on clarity, reliability, or efficiency.
- **Method 1** (Weighted average approach) is more general and directly uses the concept of averages, making it applicable to similar problems involving mixtures. It is slightly more algebra-intensive but reliable.
- **Method 2** (Proportional difference approach) is faster and simpler for this specific problem, as it directly uses the differences in densities. However, it may not generalize as well to more complex scenarios.

### Step 5: Choose the better method and use it to solve the problem, showing all steps.
- Both methods yield the same result, but Method 1 is more robust for general use. Using Method 1:
  \[
  \frac{10x + 5y}{x + y} = 9 \quad \Rightarrow \quad x = 4y \quad \Rightarrow \quad \text{Ratio of gold to copper} = 4 : 1
  \]

### Step 6: Double-check the solution for errors or unreasonable results.
- Verify the weighted average:
  \[
  \frac{10(4) + 5(1)}{4 + 1} = \frac{40 + 5}{5} = \frac{45}{5} = 9
  \]
- The calculation checks out, and the result is reasonable.

The answer is **C) 4:1**.

Q: Shalu started a business by investing Rs.50,000. After six months , Nitesh joined her with a capital of Rs.80,000. After 3 years , they earned a profit of Rs. 24,500. What was Shalu's share in the profit?
Options:
A)Rs 9423
B)Rs 10250
C)Rs 10500
D)Rs 14000
E)None of these
A_model:
### Step 1: Clearly state all given information and assumptions.
- Shalu started a business by investing Rs. 50,000.
- Nitesh joined after 6 months with a capital of Rs. 80,000.
- The business ran for 3 years (36 months total).
- The total profit earned was Rs. 24,500.
- Profit is divided in proportion to the capital invested and the duration of investment.
- We assume no other factors affect the profit distribution.

### Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem.
#### Method 1: Calculate the ratio of their investments weighted by time.
- Determine the effective capital contribution of each person by multiplying their investment by the number of months they invested.
- Use the ratio of their effective contributions to divide the profit.

#### Method 2: Use a step-by-step proportional approach.
- Calculate Shalu's contribution to the profit based on her investment and time.
- Calculate Nitesh's contribution similarly.
- Verify that the sum of their shares equals the total profit.

### Step 3: Work through both methods step by step.
#### Method 1: Calculate the ratio of investments weighted by time.
1. Shalu invested Rs. 50,000 for the full 36 months.
   - Effective contribution = Rs. 50,000 × 36 = Rs. 18,00,000.
2. Nitesh invested Rs. 80,000 for 30 months (joined after 6 months).
   - Effective contribution = Rs. 80,000 × 30 = Rs. 24,00,000.
3. Total effective contribution = Rs. 18,00,000 + Rs. 24,00,000 = Rs. 42,00,000.
4. Ratio of Shalu's contribution to Nitesh's contribution:
   - Shalu:Nitesh = 18,00,000:24,00,000 = 3:4.
5. Divide the profit of Rs. 24,500 in the ratio 3:4:
   - Shalu's share = (3/7) × Rs. 24,500 = Rs. 10,500.
   - Nitesh's share = (4/7) × Rs. 24,500 = Rs. 14,000.

#### Method 2: Use a step-by-step proportional approach.
1. Calculate Shalu's contribution to the profit:
   - Shalu's effective contribution = Rs. 50,000 × 36 = Rs. 18,00,000.
   - Total effective contribution = Rs. 18,00,000 + Rs. 24,00,000 = Rs. 42,00,000.
   - Shalu's proportion = Rs. 18,00,000 / Rs. 42,00,000 = 3/7.
   - Shalu's share of the profit = (3/7) × Rs. 24,500 = Rs. 10,500.
2. Calculate Nitesh's contribution to the profit:
   - Nitesh's effective contribution = Rs. 80,000 × 30 = Rs. 24,00,000.
   - Nitesh's proportion = Rs. 24,00,000 / Rs. 42,00,000 = 4/7.
   - Nitesh's share of the profit = (4/7) × Rs. 24,500 = Rs. 14,000.
3. Verify the total profit:
   - Shalu's share + Nitesh's share = Rs. 10,500 + Rs. 14,000 = Rs. 24,500.

### Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods.
- Both methods yield the same result.
- Method 1 is more direct and efficient, as it focuses on calculating the ratio first and then dividing the profit.
- Method 2 involves more intermediate steps but provides a detailed breakdown of contributions.

### Step 5: Choose the better method and solve.
- Method 1 is better due to its simplicity and clarity.
- Solution: Shalu's share = Rs. 10,500.

### Step 6: Double-check the solution for errors or unreasonable results.
- Verify the ratio calculation: 3:4 is correct based on effective contributions.
- Verify the profit division: (3/7) × Rs. 24,500 = Rs. 10,500.
- Total profit matches: Rs. 10,500 + Rs. 14,000 = Rs. 24,500.

The answer is **C**.

C
Correct:

Q: In a regular week, there are 5 working days and for each day, the working hours are 8. A man gets Rs.2.40 per hour for regular work and Rs.3.20 per hours for overtime. If he earns Rs.432 in 4 weeks, then how many hours does he work for?
Options:
A)160
B)175
C)180
D)195
E)185
A_model:
### Step 1: Clearly state all given information and assumptions.
- A regular week has 5 working days, and each day has 8 working hours.  
- Regular hourly wage: Rs. 2.40.  
- Overtime hourly wage: Rs. 3.20.  
- The man earns Rs. 432 in 4 weeks.  
- We need to calculate the total number of hours worked (regular + overtime).  
- Assume no deductions, taxes, or other factors affecting earnings.  

### Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem.
#### Method 1:  
1. Calculate the total regular hours worked in 4 weeks.  
2. Calculate the earnings from regular hours.  
3. Subtract regular earnings from total earnings to find overtime earnings.  
4. Use the overtime hourly rate to calculate overtime hours.  
5. Add regular hours and overtime hours to find total hours worked.  

#### Method 2:  
1. Assume the man works only regular hours initially and calculate the total earnings.  
2. Compare the calculated earnings to the actual earnings (Rs. 432).  
3. Determine the difference, which represents overtime earnings.  
4. Use the overtime hourly rate to calculate overtime hours.  
5. Add regular hours and overtime hours to find total hours worked.  

### Step 3: Work through both methods step by step.
#### Method 1:
1. **Calculate regular hours in 4 weeks:**  
   - Regular hours per week = 5 days × 8 hours/day = 40 hours.  
   - Regular hours in 4 weeks = 40 hours/week × 4 weeks = 160 hours.  

2. **Calculate earnings from regular hours:**  
   - Regular hourly wage = Rs. 2.40.  
   - Regular earnings = 160 hours × Rs. 2.40/hour = Rs. 384.  

3. **Find overtime earnings:**  
   - Total earnings = Rs. 432.  
   - Overtime earnings = Total earnings - Regular earnings = Rs. 432 - Rs. 384 = Rs. 48.  

4. **Calculate overtime hours:**  
   - Overtime hourly wage = Rs. 3.20.  
   - Overtime hours = Overtime earnings ÷ Overtime hourly wage = Rs. 48 ÷ Rs. 3.20 = 15 hours.  

5. **Find total hours worked:**  
   - Total hours = Regular hours + Overtime hours = 160 hours + 15 hours = 175 hours.  

#### Method 2:
1. **Assume only regular hours initially:**  
   - Regular hours in 4 weeks = 160 hours.  
   - Regular earnings = 160 hours × Rs. 2.40/hour = Rs. 384.  

2. **Compare calculated earnings to actual earnings:**  
   - Total earnings = Rs. 432.  
   - Difference = Rs. 432 - Rs. 384 = Rs. 48 (overtime earnings).  

3. **Calculate overtime hours:**  
   - Overtime hourly wage = Rs. 3.20.  
   - Overtime hours = Rs. 48 ÷ Rs. 3.20 = 15 hours.  

4. **Find total hours worked:**  
   - Total hours = Regular hours + Overtime hours = 160 hours + 15 hours = 175 hours.  

### Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods.
- Both methods yield the same result (175 hours).  
- Method 1 is slightly more structured and breaks the problem into smaller steps, making it easier to follow.  
- Method 2 is more intuitive but assumes regular hours first, which might not always be clear in more complex problems.  

### Step 5: Choose the better method and solve.
- Method 1 is chosen for its clarity and structured approach.  
- Solution:  
   - Regular hours = 160 hours.  
   - Regular earnings = Rs. 384.  
   - Overtime earnings = Rs. 48.  
   - Overtime hours = 15 hours.  
   - Total hours = 160 + 15 = 175 hours.  

### Step 6: Double-check the solution.
- Regular earnings = 160 × Rs. 2.40 = Rs. 384.  
- Overtime earnings = Rs. 48.  
- Overtime hours = Rs. 48 ÷ Rs. 3.20 = 15 hours.  
- Total hours = 160 + 15 = 175 hours.  

The answer is **B) 175**.

B
Correct:
B

Q: A 90-liter solution of cool-drink is made from 5% jasmine water. If 8 liters of jasmine and 2 liters of water were added to the solution, what percent of the solution is jasmine?
Options:
A)6.5%
B)8.5%
C)10.5%
D)12.5%
E)14.5%
A_model:
### Step 1: Clearly state all given information and any assumptions.
- The initial solution is 90 liters, and it contains 5% jasmine water.
- This means the initial amount of jasmine water is \( 90 \times 0.05 = 4.5 \) liters.
- 8 liters of jasmine water and 2 liters of plain water are added to the solution.
- The total volume of the solution after the addition is \( 90 + 8 + 2 = 100 \) liters.
- We need to calculate the percentage of jasmine water in the new solution.

### Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem.
#### Method 1: Direct calculation of jasmine water percentage.
- Add the initial jasmine water to the added jasmine water to find the total jasmine water.
- Divide the total jasmine water by the total solution volume and multiply by 100 to find the percentage.

#### Method 2: Proportional reasoning.
- Calculate the ratio of jasmine water to the total solution volume after the addition.
- Use this ratio to determine the percentage of jasmine water.

### Step 3: Work through all intermediate steps in detail.
#### Method 1: Direct calculation.
1. Initial jasmine water: \( 4.5 \) liters.
2. Added jasmine water: \( 8 \) liters.
3. Total jasmine water: \( 4.5 + 8 = 12.5 \) liters.
4. Total solution volume: \( 90 + 8 + 2 = 100 \) liters.
5. Jasmine water percentage:  
   \[
   \text{Percentage} = \left( \frac{\text{Total jasmine water}}{\text{Total solution volume}} \right) \times 100
   \]
   \[
   \text{Percentage} = \left( \frac{12.5}{100} \right) \times 100 = 12.5\%.
   \]

#### Method 2: Proportional reasoning.
1. Initial jasmine water: \( 4.5 \) liters.
2. Added jasmine water: \( 8 \) liters.
3. Total jasmine water: \( 4.5 + 8 = 12.5 \) liters.
4. Total solution volume: \( 90 + 8 + 2 = 100 \) liters.
5. Ratio of jasmine water to total solution volume:  
   \[
   \text{Ratio} = \frac{\text{Total jasmine water}}{\text{Total solution volume}} = \frac{12.5}{100}.
   \]
6. Convert the ratio to a percentage:  
   \[
   \text{Percentage} = \text{Ratio} \times 100 = \frac{12.5}{100} \times 100 = 12.5\%.
   \]

### Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods.
- Both methods yield the same result and are equally reliable.
- Method 1 is slightly more straightforward because it directly calculates the percentage without introducing ratios.

### Step 5: Choose the better method and solve.
- Method 1 is chosen for its simplicity and clarity.
- Solution: The percentage of jasmine water in the new solution is \( 12.5\% \).

### Step 6: Double-check the solution for errors or unreasonable results.
- Total jasmine water: \( 4.5 + 8 = 12.5 \) liters.
- Total solution volume: \( 90 + 8 + 2 = 100 \) liters.
- Jasmine water percentage: \( \frac{12.5}{100} \times 100 = 12.5\% \).
- The calculations are correct and reasonable.

The answer is D.

D
Correct:

Q: Evaluate combination
16C2
Options:
A)120
B)1000
C)10
D)1
E)0
A_model:
Step 1: Clearly state all given information and any assumptions.  
- We are tasked with evaluating the combination \( 16C2 \), which represents the number of ways to choose 2 items from a set of 16 items.  
- The formula for combinations is:  
  \[
  nCk = \frac{n!}{k!(n-k)!}
  \]  
  where \( n \) is the total number of items, \( k \) is the number of items to choose, and \( ! \) denotes factorial.  
- Here, \( n = 16 \) and \( k = 2 \).  

Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem, briefly outlining the logic of each.  
**Method 1:** Use the combination formula directly:  
\[
16C2 = \frac{16!}{2!(16-2)!} = \frac{16!}{2! \cdot 14!}.
\]  
Simplify by canceling \( 14! \) in the numerator and denominator, leaving only the terms \( 16 \cdot 15 \) in the numerator.  

**Method 2:** Use a step-by-step approach to calculate the numerator and denominator separately:  
- Numerator: Calculate \( 16 \cdot 15 \).  
- Denominator: Calculate \( 2! = 2 \).  
- Divide the numerator by the denominator.  

Step 3: For each method, work through all intermediate steps in detail, showing calculations, checks, and potential pitfalls.  

**Method 1:**  
\[
16C2 = \frac{16!}{2!(16-2)!} = \frac{16!}{2! \cdot 14!}.
\]  
Cancel \( 14! \) in the numerator and denominator:  
\[
16C2 = \frac{16 \cdot 15 \cdot 14!}{2! \cdot 14!}.
\]  
Simplify further:  
\[
16C2 = \frac{16 \cdot 15}{2!}.
\]  
Calculate \( 2! = 2 \):  
\[
16C2 = \frac{16 \cdot 15}{2}.
\]  
Multiply the numerator:  
\[
16 \cdot 15 = 240.
\]  
Divide by 2:  
\[
240 \div 2 = 120.
\]  

**Method 2:**  
Numerator: Calculate \( 16 \cdot 15 \):  
\[
16 \cdot 15 = 240.
\]  
Denominator: Calculate \( 2! = 2 \).  
Divide the numerator by the denominator:  
\[
240 \div 2 = 120.
\]  

Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods—discussing which is better based on clarity, reliability, or efficiency.  
- Both methods yield the same result.  
- Method 1 is more formal and directly uses the combination formula, which is useful for understanding the mathematical process.  
- Method 2 is simpler and faster for small values of \( n \) and \( k \).  

Step 5: Choose the better method and use it to solve the problem, showing all steps.  
- Method 2 is better for this problem due to its simplicity.  
- Solution:  
\[
16C2 = \frac{16 \cdot 15}{2} = \frac{240}{2} = 120.
\]  

Step 6: Double-check the solution for errors or unreasonable results.  
- Recalculate using Method 1:  
\[
16C2 = \frac{16 \cdot 15}{2} = 120.
\]  
- The result is consistent.  

The answer is **A) 120**.

A
Correct:

Q: Find the average of first 30 natural numbers.
Options:
A)A)13.5
B)B)20.5
C)C)18.6
D)D)15.5
E)E)12.3
A_model:
### Step 1: Clearly state all given information and any assumptions.
- We are tasked with finding the average of the first 30 natural numbers.
- The first 30 natural numbers are: 1, 2, 3, ..., 30.
- The formula for the average of a set of numbers is:  
  \[
  \text{Average} = \frac{\text{Sum of all numbers}}{\text{Total count of numbers}}
  \]
- We assume the numbers are consecutive and start from 1.

---

### Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem, briefly outlining the logic of each.

**Method 1: Use the formula for the sum of the first \(n\) natural numbers.**  
- The sum of the first \(n\) natural numbers is given by the formula:  
  \[
  S = \frac{n(n+1)}{2}
  \]
- Calculate the sum for \(n = 30\), then divide by 30 to find the average.

**Method 2: Use the property of evenly spaced numbers.**  
- For any sequence of evenly spaced numbers, the average is equal to the midpoint of the first and last numbers.  
- The first number is 1, and the last number is 30. The average is:  
  \[
  \text{Average} = \frac{\text{First number} + \text{Last number}}{2}
  \]

---

### Step 3: For each method, work through all intermediate steps in detail, showing calculations, checks, and potential pitfalls.

#### **Method 1: Use the sum formula.**
1. Use the formula for the sum of the first \(n\) natural numbers:  
   \[
   S = \frac{n(n+1)}{2}
   \]
2. Substitute \(n = 30\):  
   \[
   S = \frac{30(30+1)}{2} = \frac{30 \cdot 31}{2} = \frac{930}{2} = 465
   \]
3. Divide the sum by the total count of numbers (\(n = 30\)) to find the average:  
   \[
   \text{Average} = \frac{S}{n} = \frac{465}{30} = 15.5
   \]

#### **Method 2: Use the midpoint property.**
1. The first number in the sequence is 1, and the last number is 30.
2. The average of evenly spaced numbers is the midpoint:  
   \[
   \text{Average} = \frac{\text{First number} + \text{Last number}}{2}
   \]
3. Substitute the values:  
   \[
   \text{Average} = \frac{1 + 30}{2} = \frac{31}{2} = 15.5
   \]

---

### Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods.
- **Method 1** involves using the sum formula and dividing by the count. It is reliable and works for any sequence of natural numbers.
- **Method 2** is simpler and faster for evenly spaced numbers, as it avoids calculating the entire sum.
- Both methods yield the same result, but **Method 2** is more efficient for this specific problem.

---

### Step 5: Choose the better method and use it to solve the problem.
- **Method 2** is chosen for its simplicity and efficiency.
- Using Method 2:  
  \[
  \text{Average} = \frac{1 + 30}{2} = 15.5
  \]

---

### Step 6: Double-check the solution for errors or unreasonable results.
- Verify using Method 1:  
  \[
  S = \frac{30(30+1)}{2} = 465, \quad \text{Average} = \frac{465}{30} = 15.5
  \]
- Both methods confirm the result is correct.

---

The answer is **D) 15.5**.
Extracted Option:
D
Correct:
D

Q: In a lottery, there are 10 prizes and 15 blanks. A lottery is drawn at random. What is the probability of getting a prize?
Options:
A)1/10
B)2/5
C)2/7
D)5/7
E)None
A_model:
Step 1: Clearly state all given information and any assumptions.  
- There are 10 prizes and 15 blanks in the lottery.  
- A lottery is drawn at random.  
- The total number of outcomes is the sum of prizes and blanks: 10 + 15 = 25.  
- The probability of getting a prize is the ratio of favorable outcomes (prizes) to total outcomes.  

Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem, briefly outlining the logic of each.  
Method 1: Use the formula for probability: \( P(\text{prize}) = \frac{\text{number of prizes}}{\text{total outcomes}} \).  
Method 2: Express the probability as a fraction and simplify it by finding the greatest common divisor (GCD) of the numerator and denominator.  

Step 3: For each method, work through all intermediate steps in detail, showing calculations, checks, and potential pitfalls.  

**Method 1:**  
- Number of prizes = 10.  
- Total outcomes = 25.  
- Probability of getting a prize:  
  \[
  P(\text{prize}) = \frac{10}{25}.
  \]  
- Simplify the fraction:  
  \[
  \frac{10}{25} = \frac{2}{5}.
  \]  
- The probability of getting a prize is \( \frac{2}{5} \).  

**Method 2:**  
- Start with the fraction \( \frac{10}{25} \).  
- Find the GCD of 10 and 25:  
  - Prime factorization of 10: \( 2 \times 5 \).  
  - Prime factorization of 25: \( 5 \times 5 \).  
  - Common factor: \( 5 \).  
- Divide both numerator and denominator by the GCD (5):  
  \[
  \frac{10}{25} = \frac{10 \div 5}{25 \div 5} = \frac{2}{5}.
  \]  
- The probability of getting a prize is \( \frac{2}{5} \).  

Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods—discussing which is better based on clarity, reliability, or efficiency.  
- Both methods yield the same result.  
- Method 1 is more direct and efficient, as it avoids explicitly finding the GCD.  
- Method 2 is useful for understanding the simplification process but involves extra steps.  

Step 5: Choose the better method and use it to solve the problem, showing all steps.  
- Method 1 is better due to its simplicity and efficiency.  
- Solution:  
  \[
  P(\text{prize}) = \frac{10}{25} = \frac{2}{5}.
  \]  

Step 6: Double-check the solution for errors or unreasonable results.  
- Total outcomes = 25, favorable outcomes = 10.  
- Probability = \( \frac{10}{25} = \frac{2}{5} \).  
- The result is reasonable and checks out.  

The answer is **B) 2/5**.
Extracted Option:
B
Correct:
B

Q: In the Clock Game, part of the game show The Price Is Right, a contestant must
guess the price (rounded to the nearest dollar) of a prize which is worth less than $2,000. After
each guess, the contestant is told whether her guess was correct, too low, or too high. Assume
that the contestant is mathematically savvy but has no idea how much the prize is worth.
With how many guesses is she guaranteed to win the prize?
Options:
A)10
B)11
C)12
D)13
E)1997
A_model:
### Step 1: Clearly state all given information and any assumptions.
- The contestant must guess the price of a prize worth less than $2,000.
- After each guess, the contestant is told whether the guess is correct, too low, or too high.
- The contestant is mathematically savvy but has no prior knowledge of the prize's value.
- The goal is to determine the maximum number of guesses required to guarantee finding the correct price.

**Assumptions:**
- The contestant uses an optimal strategy to minimize the number of guesses.
- The optimal strategy is a binary search, where the contestant splits the range of possible prices in half with each guess.

### Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem, briefly outlining the logic of each.
**Method 1: Binary Search Calculation**
- In binary search, the range of possible values is halved with each guess. The number of guesses required to guarantee finding the correct price is the smallest integer \( n \) such that \( 2^n \geq \text{range of possible prices} \). Here, the range is 2,000 (from 0 to 1,999).

**Method 2: Trial and Error with Guessing**
- Start with the full range (0 to 1,999) and simulate the process of halving the range with each guess. Count the number of guesses required to reduce the range to a single value.

### Step 3: For each method, work through all intermediate steps in detail, showing calculations, checks, and potential pitfalls.

#### **Method 1: Binary Search Calculation**
1. The range of possible prices is 2,000 (from 0 to 1,999).
2. The number of guesses required is the smallest \( n \) such that \( 2^n \geq 2000 \).
3. Compute powers of 2:
   - \( 2^{10} = 1024 \) (too small)
   - \( 2^{11} = 2048 \) (sufficient)
4. Therefore, \( n = 11 \) guesses are required to guarantee finding the correct price.

#### **Method 2: Trial and Error with Guessing**
1. Start with the range 0 to 1,999.
2. First guess: Split the range in half (e.g., guess 999). This reduces the range to 1,000 values.
3. Second guess: Split the remaining range in half again. This reduces the range to 500 values.
4. Continue halving the range with each guess:
   - After 3 guesses: Range = 250 values.
   - After 4 guesses: Range = 125 values.
   - After 5 guesses: Range = 63 values.
   - After 6 guesses: Range = 31 values.
   - After 7 guesses: Range = 15 values.
   - After 8 guesses: Range = 7 values.
   - After 9 guesses: Range = 3 values.
   - After 10 guesses: Range = 1 value.
5. The process confirms that 11 guesses are required to guarantee finding the correct price.

### Step 4: Evaluate and compare the two methods.
- **Method 1 (Binary Search Calculation)** is more efficient and mathematically rigorous. It directly calculates the number of guesses required without simulating the process.
- **Method 2 (Trial and Error)** is less efficient but provides a step-by-step confirmation of the result. It is useful for verifying the calculation but is more time-consuming.

### Step 5: Choose the better method and use it to solve the problem, showing all steps.
- Method 1 is better due to its clarity and efficiency.
- Using binary search, the smallest \( n \) such that \( 2^n \geq 2000 \) is \( n = 11 \).

### Step 6: Double-check the solution for errors or unreasonable results.
- Verify \( 2^{10} = 1024 \) and \( 2^{11} = 2048 \). Since \( 2048 \geq 2000 \), 11 guesses are sufficient.
- The result is reasonable because binary search is the optimal strategy for minimizing guesses.

The answer is **B) 11**.
Extracted Option:
B

- We are tasked with finding the total number of words that can be formed using all the letters of "BIHAR."  
- Since all letters are distinct, there are no repetitions.  
- The order of the letters matters, as we are forming permutations.

Step 2: Propose two different methods to solve the problem, briefly outlining the logic of each.  
**Method 1:** Use the formula for permutations of distinct objects. The total number of permutations of \( n \) distinct objects is \( n! \).  
**Method 2:** Use step-by-step reasoning to calculate the number of arrangements by considering the choices for each position in the word.

Step 3: For each method, work through all intermediate steps in detail, showing calculations, checks, and potential pitfalls.  
**Method 1:**  
- The word "BIHAR" has 5 distinct letters.  
- The total number of permutations is calculated as \( n! \), where \( n = 5 \).  
- \( 5! = 5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2 \times 1 = 120 \).  
- This method is straightforward and relies on the formula for permutations.  

**Method 2:**  
- Step-by-step reasoning:  
  - For the first position, there are 5 choices (B, I, H, A, R).  
  - For the second position, there are 4 remaining choices.  
  - For the third position, there are 3 remaining choices.  
  - For the fourth position, there are 2 remaining choices.  
  - For the fifth position, there is 1 remaining choice.  
- Multiply the number of choices for each position:  
  \( 5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2 \times 1 = 120 \).  
- This method confirms the result obtained using the formula.

- **Method 1** is more efficient and concise, as it directly applies the formula for permutations.  
- **Method 2** is more detailed and intuitive, breaking down the problem step by step.  
- Both methods are reliable and yield the same result, but Method 1 is faster for this type of problem.

- Method 1 is chosen for its efficiency.  
- Solution: \( 5! = 120 \).
